(\frac{w}{3})^{\alpha}\leq y\leq w^{\alpha}-C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}
. Notice that in this region it holds that
y^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\leq(y+C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}})^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\leq w\leq 3y^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}
. We remember that
C_{0}
is of order
A^{\frac{1}{m-1}}
. Thus

\displaystyle\int_{\{(\frac{w}{3})^{\alpha}\leq y\leq w^{\alpha}-C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}\}}w^{k}P(y,w,t)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\leq\int^{3y^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}}_{(y+C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}})^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}}\frac{C(\alpha,\gamma,b,m)A^{3}w^{k}\epsilon}{(1+w^{b})|y-w^{\alpha}|}\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(\alpha,\gamma,b,m)A^{3}\epsilon y^{\frac{k}{\alpha}}}{1+y^{\frac{b}{\alpha}}}\int^{3y^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}}_{(y+C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}})^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}}\frac{1}{|y-w^{\alpha}|}\textup{d}w.

We make the change of variables
z=y-w^{\alpha}
and obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon y^{\frac{k}{\alpha}}}{1+y^{\frac{b}{\alpha}}}\int^{3y^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}}_{(y+C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}})^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}}\frac{1}{|y-w^{\alpha}|}\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(\alpha)\epsilon y^{\frac{k+1-\alpha}{\alpha}}}{1+y^{\frac{b}{\alpha}}}\int_{-(3^{\alpha}-1)y}^{-C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}}\frac{\textup{d}z}{|z|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(\alpha)\epsilon y^{\frac{k+1-\alpha}{\alpha}}}{1+y^{\frac{b}{\alpha}}}\big[\frac{t}{\epsilon}+|\ln{y}|\big]\leq\frac{C(\alpha)}{1+y^{\frac{b-k-1}{\alpha}}},

where in the last inequality we used the fact that
t\leq 1
.

•

We now look at the contribution of the region
\{0\leq y\leq(\frac{w}{3})^{\alpha}\}
. In this region, we have that
|y-w^{\alpha}|=w^{\alpha}-y
and
\big[1-\frac{1}{3^{\alpha}}\big]w^{\alpha}\leq w^{\alpha}-y\leq w^{\alpha}
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{\{0\leq y\leq(\frac{w}{3})^{\alpha}\}}w^{k}P(y,w,t)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{0\leq y\leq(\frac{w}{3})^{\alpha}\}}\frac{C(\alpha,\gamma,b,m)Ae^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}}{1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}(w^{\alpha}-y)^{m}}\frac{w^{k}}{1+w^{b}}\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{0\leq y\leq(\frac{w}{3})^{\alpha}\}}\frac{C(\alpha,\gamma,b,m)Ae^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}}{1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}w^{\alpha m}}\frac{w^{k}}{1+w^{b}}\textup{d}w.

We make the change of variable
z=e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}w^{\alpha}
and obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{\{0\leq y\leq(\frac{w}{3})^{\alpha}\}}w^{k}P(y,w,t)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha,\gamma,b,m)A\int_{(3^{\alpha}e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}y,\infty)}\frac{(ze^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}})^{\frac{k+1-\alpha}{\alpha}}\textup{d}z}{1+z^{m}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha,\gamma,b,m)A\int_{(3^{\alpha}e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}y,\infty)}\frac{z^{\frac{k+1-\alpha}{\alpha}}\textup{d}z}{1+z^{m}}\leq\frac{C(\alpha,\gamma,b,m)A}{1+y^{m-\frac{k+1-\alpha}{\alpha}}},

This concludes our proof in the case
y\geq 0
since
m\geq\frac{b}{\alpha}
. If
y\leq 0
, by making the change of variable
z=e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(w^{\alpha}-y)
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\int w^{k}P(y,w,t)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\leq\int_{(0,\infty)}\frac{C(\alpha,\gamma,b,m)Aw^{k}e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}}{(1+w^{b})(1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}|y-w^{\alpha}|^{m})}\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\leq\int_{(e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}|y|,\infty)}\frac{C(\alpha,\gamma,b,m)A}{1+|z|^{m}}\textup{d}z\leq\frac{C(\alpha,\gamma,b,m)A}{1+(e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}|y|)^{m-1}}.

This concludes our proof.
∎

We continue by analyzing properties of the transport equation (
4.2
). Define
d
via

\left\{\begin{aligned} d&=\bigg\lfloor\frac{b-2}{\alpha}\bigg\rfloor-1;&\textup{ if }\bigg\lfloor\frac{b-2}{\alpha}\bigg\rfloor&\textup{ odd; }\\
d&=\bigg\lfloor\frac{b-2}{\alpha}\bigg\rfloor;&\textup{ if }\bigg\lfloor\frac{b-2}{\alpha}\bigg\rfloor&\textup{ even, }\end{aligned}\right.

(4.7)

where
\lfloor t\rfloor
denotes the floor function. We use the method of characteristics in (
4.2
)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}{Y_{\epsilon}}(y,v,t)&=-\frac{1}{\epsilon}({V_{\epsilon}}^{\alpha}-Y),&\qquad{Y_{\epsilon}}(y,v,0)&=y\leq\big(\frac{v}{3}\big)^{\alpha}\,,\\
\partial_{t}{V_{\epsilon}}(y,v,t)&=-\frac{{L}{V_{\epsilon}}^{\gamma}\xi(V_{\epsilon})}{1+|{Y_{\epsilon}}|^{d}},&\qquad{V_{\epsilon}}(y,v,0)&=v.\end{aligned}\right.

(4.8)

Notice that for
b>4
then
d>1
and even. It immediately follows from (
4.8
) that

\displaystyle\frac{\textup{d}V_{\epsilon}}{\textup{d}Y_{\epsilon}}=\frac{\epsilon LV_{\epsilon}^{\gamma}\xi(V_{\epsilon})}{(1+|Y_{\epsilon}|^{d})(V_{\epsilon}^{\alpha}-Y_{\epsilon})}.

(4.9)

Notice that this is different from the analysis of the characteristics performed in
[
9
]
since in that case we could make use of the separation of variables in (
4.9
) since the transport term in the space variable contributed with
V_{\epsilon}^{\alpha}
instead of
V_{\epsilon}^{\alpha}-Y_{\epsilon}
. We thus have to apply different methods than in
[
9
]
to prove the desired estimates in our case.

4.2
Properties of the characteristics of the equation satisfied by the supersolution

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
\gamma\in(1,1+\alpha)
, and
d>1
even. Let
L>0
and let
Y_{\epsilon},V_{\epsilon}
be as in (
4.8
). Then there exists a sufficiently small
\epsilon_{1}\in(0,1)
, which depends on
L
, such that for all
t\geq 0
and
\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{1}
, the following estimates hold for all
v>0
and
y\leq\big(\frac{v}{3}\big)^{\alpha}
.

\displaystyle\frac{9}{10}v

\displaystyle\leq V_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\leq v;

(4.10)

\displaystyle Y_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)

\displaystyle\leq\big(\frac{v}{3}\big)^{\alpha};

(4.11)

\displaystyle e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})

\displaystyle\leq Y_{\epsilon}-V_{\epsilon}^{\alpha};

(4.12)

\displaystyle Y_{\epsilon}-V_{\epsilon}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}v^{\alpha}).

(4.13)

Remark 4.5
.

We will later fix
L
in (
4.37
) to depend on
A
and other constants depending only on the parameters
\alpha,\gamma,b,m
. Thus
\epsilon_{1}
will depend on
A
.

Proof of Proposition
4.4
.

In order to simplify the notation, we replace the notation of the pair
(Y_{\epsilon},V_{\epsilon})
in (
4.8
) with
(Y,V)
, while keeping in mind the dependence on
\epsilon
. Moreover, we will assume in all the following that
v\geq 1
and will work with the following system of ODE’s.

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}{Y}(y,v,t)&=-\frac{1}{\epsilon}({V}^{\alpha}-Y),&\qquad{Y}(y,v,0)&=y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}\,,\\
\partial_{t}{V}(y,v,t)&=-\frac{{L}{V}^{\gamma}}{1+|{Y}|^{d}},&\qquad{V}(y,v,0)&=v.\end{aligned}\right.

(4.14)

This is since there is nothing to prove in the case
v\leq\frac{1}{2}
since by the definition of
\xi
in (
4.1
), it holds that
\partial_{t}V_{\epsilon}=0
when
v\leq\frac{1}{2}
. Moreover, assume that (
4.10
)-(
4.13
) hold for
v\geq\frac{1}{2}
and for (
4.14
). We notice that the following inequalities hold. We have that
\partial_{t}V_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)=-\frac{LV_{\epsilon}^{\gamma}\xi(V_{\epsilon})}{1+|Y_{\epsilon}|^{d}}\geq-\frac{LV_{\epsilon}^{\gamma}}{1+|Y_{\epsilon}|^{d}}
and that
\partial_{t}V_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\leq 0
. Thus, (
4.10
) follows. If (
4.10
) holds, then (
4.11
) follows from (
4.8
). (
4.12
)-(
4.13
) follow from (
4.10
)-(
4.11
). Thus, it suffices to analyze the system (
4.14
) with
v\geq\frac{1}{2}
. We assume in addition that
v\geq 1
for simplicity of notation.

From (
4.8
), we have that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}{V}(y,v,t)=-\frac{{L}{V}^{\gamma}}{1+|{Y}|^{d}}\geq-LV^{\gamma}.

(4.15)

Thus, there exists a time
t_{1}:=t_{1}(v)
sufficiently small, which is independent of
\epsilon
but dependent on
v
, such that

\displaystyle\frac{9}{10}v\leq V(y,v,s)\leq v,

for all
0\leq s\leq t_{1}
. This implies that for all
s\leq t_{1}
it holds that

\displaystyle\partial_{s}{Y}(y,v,s)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\epsilon}(Y-{V}^{\alpha})\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon}(Y-\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}{v}^{\alpha}).

(4.16)

We analyze the sign of the following ODE.

\displaystyle\partial_{z}{\overline{Y}}(y,v,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\overline{Y}-\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}{v}^{\alpha}),\quad\overline{Y}(y,v,0)=y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}.

(4.17)

Since
\overline{Y}=\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}{v}^{\alpha}
is the zero point of (
4.17
) and
\overline{Y}(y,v,0)=y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}\leq\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}{v}^{\alpha}
, then we have that
\overline{Y}(y,v,z)\leq\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}{v}^{\alpha}
, for all
z\geq 0
and furthermore
\overline{Y}(y,v,z)\leq y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}
, for all
z\geq 0
.

By (
4.16
) and using a comparison principle, this implies that
Y(y,v,s)\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}
, for all
s\leq t_{1}
and in particular
Y(y,v,t_{1})\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}
.
We thus have that
V(s)\geq\frac{9}{10}v
and
Y(s)\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}
, for all
s\leq t_{1}
. Then (
4.16
) becomes

\displaystyle\partial_{s}Y\leq\frac{v^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}\bigg[\frac{1}{3^{\alpha}}-\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}\bigg]\leq-\frac{cv^{\alpha}}{\epsilon},

(4.18)

for some
c>0
.

i)

Assume first that
Y(y,v,0)=y\in[0,(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}]
and
Y(y,v,t_{1})<0
.

We have that for any
y\in\mathbb{R}
and
v>0
there exists
\tau:=\tau(y,v)\in[0,t_{1})
such that
Y(y,v,\tau(y,v))=0
. Thus, for all
s\in[0,t_{1}]
, it holds that

\displaystyle|Y(y,v,s)|=|Y(y,v,s)-Y(y,v,\tau(y,v))|\geq\frac{cv^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}|s-\tau(y,v)|.

Let
s\in[0,t_{1}]
. Since
\frac{9}{10}v\leq V\leq v
, it holds that

\displaystyle\partial_{s}{V}(y,v,s)=-\frac{{L}{V}^{\gamma}}{1+|{Y}|^{d}}\geq-\frac{{L}{V}^{\gamma}}{1+\frac{cv^{\alpha d}}{\epsilon^{d}}|s-\tau(y,v)|^{d}}\geq-\frac{C{L}{v}^{\gamma}}{1+\frac{cv^{\alpha d}}{\epsilon^{d}}|s-\tau(y,v)|^{d}}.

In other words, we have that

\displaystyle V(y,v,s)-v\geq-v^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{CL\textup{d}z}{1+\frac{cv^{\alpha d}}{\epsilon^{d}}|z-\tau(y,v)|^{d}}.

(4.19)

By making the change of variables
\xi=\frac{v^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}(z-\tau(y,v))
in (
4.19
), it follows that

\displaystyle V(y,v,s)\geq v-\epsilon v^{\gamma-\alpha}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{CL\textup{d}\xi}{1+c|\xi|^{d}}\geq v\bigg[1-CL\epsilon v^{\gamma-\alpha-1}\bigg].

(4.20)

Since
v\geq 1
and
\gamma<\alpha+1
, there exists
\epsilon_{1}\in(0,1)
sufficiently small, depending on
L
, but independent of
v
such that
\bigg[1-CL\epsilon v^{\gamma-\alpha-1}\bigg]\geq\frac{19}{20}
and thus

\displaystyle V(y,v,s)\geq\frac{19}{20}v,

(4.21)

for all
s\in[0,t_{1}]
and all
\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{1}
.

From (
4.21
), (
4.15
), and by continuity, we deduce that there exists
t_{2}:=t_{2}(v)>t_{1}
such that

\displaystyle\frac{9}{10}v\leq V(y,v,s)\leq v,\textup{ for all }s\in[t_{1},t_{2}].

ii)

Assume
Y(y,v,0)=y\in[0,(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}]
and
Y(y,v,t_{1})\geq 0
.

Since
Y(y,v,s)\geq Y(y,v,t_{1})\geq 0
, for all
s\in[0,t_{1}]
, it holds that

\displaystyle|Y(y,v,s)|=Y(y,v,s)\geq Y(y,v,s)-Y(y,v,t_{1})\geq|Y(y,v,s)-Y(y,v,t_{1})|\geq\frac{cv^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}|s-t_{1}|.

We can then perform similar computations as in the case
Y(y,v,0)=y\in[0,(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}]
and
Y(y,v,t_{1})<0
in order to deduce that
V(y,v,s)\geq\frac{19}{20}v,
for all
s\leq t_{1}
and all
\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{1}
.

iii)

Assume
Y(y,v,0)=y<0
.

Then for all
s\in[0,t_{1}]
, it holds by (
4.16
) that

\displaystyle Y(y,v,s)\leq-\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}\frac{v^{\alpha}s}{\epsilon}

(4.22)

and thus, as before, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\partial_{s}{V}(y,v,s)=-\frac{{L}{V}^{\gamma}}{1+|{Y}|^{d}}\geq-\frac{{L}{V}^{\gamma}}{1+\frac{cv^{\alpha d}}{\epsilon^{d}}s^{d}}\geq-\frac{C{L}{v}^{\gamma}}{1+\frac{cv^{\alpha d}}{\epsilon^{d}}s^{d}}.

As before, we obtain that

\displaystyle V(y,v,s)\geq\frac{19}{20}v,

(4.23)

for all
s\in[0,t_{1}]
and all
\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{1}
.

We are now able to iterate the argument in order to obtain that (
4.10
) holds for all
t\geq 0
. Notice that at each iteration the times
t_{i}
,
i\in\mathbb{N}
, depend on
v
, but after the iteration we obtain that the estimate (
4.10
) holds for all times
t\geq 0
. Moreover,
\epsilon_{1}
in (
4.20
) does not depend on
v
. The inequality (
4.11
) follows from (
4.10
) and (
4.16
).

By (
4.10
) and (
4.8
) it follows that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Y-\frac{Y}{\epsilon}=-\frac{V^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}\geq-\frac{v^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}

and thus

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bigg(e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}Y\bigg)\geq\partial_{t}\bigg(e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}v^{\alpha}\bigg).

Integrating in time and using the fact that
V\leq v
we obtain (
4.12
). (
4.13
) follows in a similar manner by noticing that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Y-\frac{Y}{\epsilon}=-\frac{V^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}\leq-\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}\frac{v^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}.

This concludes our proof.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

From (
4.13
) we immediately deduce that
for all
y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha},
the following inequality holds.

\displaystyle Y_{\epsilon}-V_{\epsilon}^{\alpha}\leq\bigg[\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}-\frac{1}{3^{\alpha}}\bigg]e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha}).

Proof.

For
y\leq 0
, it immediately follows that

\displaystyle Y_{\epsilon}-V_{\epsilon}^{\alpha}\leq e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}v^{\alpha})\leq\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})\leq\bigg[\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}-\frac{1}{3^{\alpha}}\bigg]e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha}).

If
y\in(0,(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha})
, then

\displaystyle Y_{\epsilon}-V_{\epsilon}^{\alpha}\leq e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}v^{\alpha})\leq-\bigg[\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}-\frac{1}{3^{\alpha}}\bigg]e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}v^{\alpha}\leq\bigg[\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}-\frac{1}{3^{\alpha}}\bigg]e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha}).

∎

4.3
Properties of the supersolution in the region
y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}

Proposition 4.7
.

Fix
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
\gamma\in(1,1+\alpha)
,
L>0
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
. Let
d>1
in (
4.7
) even and sufficiently large such that
\gamma\leq\frac{d\alpha}{d+1}+1
. Let
G_{\epsilon,L}
solve equation (
4.2
). Then there exists a sufficiently small
\epsilon_{1}\in(0,1)
such that for all
t\geq 0
and
\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{1}
, we have that for all
v>0
and
y\leq\big(\frac{v}{3}\big)^{\alpha}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon,L}(y,v,t)\leq 0.

Proof.

Let
Y_{\epsilon},V_{\epsilon}
be as in (
4.8
). We have that

\displaystyle G_{\epsilon,L}(y,v,t)=\frac{Ae^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}}{(1+V_{\epsilon}^{b})(1+|Y_{\epsilon}-V_{\epsilon}^{\alpha}|^{m})},

with
A
as in (
2.1
). As before, in order to simplify the notation, we replace the notation of the pair
(Y_{\epsilon},V_{\epsilon})
in (
4.8
) with
(Y,V)
, while keeping in mind the dependence on
\epsilon
. Moreover, from Proposition
4.4
, we know that
Y-V^{\alpha}\leq 0
, for all
t\geq 0
. Thus

\displaystyle\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon,L}(y,v,t)=-\frac{mAe^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(V^{\alpha}-Y)^{m-1}[\alpha V^{\alpha-1}\partial_{v}V-\partial_{v}Y]}{(1+V^{b})(1+(V^{\alpha}-Y)^{m})^{2}}-\frac{bAe^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}V^{b-1}\partial_{v}V}{(1+V^{b})^{2}(1+(V^{\alpha}-Y)^{m})}.

Thus, in order to prove that
\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon,L}(y,v,t)\leq 0
it suffices to prove that
\alpha V^{\alpha-1}\partial_{v}V-\partial_{v}Y\geq 0
and
\partial_{v}V\geq 0
. Fix
t>0
. We know that
V\geq 0
by Proposition
4.4
. We will prove that

\displaystyle\partial_{v}V(y,v,t)\geq 0,\textup{ for all }v>0,y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha},

and that

\displaystyle\partial_{v}Y(y,v,t)\leq 0,\textup{ for all }v>0,y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha},

thus concluding our proof. Since
\partial_{v}V(y,v,0)=1
, by continuity there exists
t_{1}>0
such that
\partial_{v}V(y,v,s)\in[\frac{1}{2},1]
, for all
s\in[0,t_{1}]
. By (
4.8
), we have that

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\partial_{v}Y(y,v,s)=\frac{\partial_{v}Y}{\epsilon}-\frac{\alpha V^{\alpha-1}\partial_{v}V}{\epsilon},\qquad\partial_{v}Y(y,v,0)=0.

In other words, using Proposition
4.4
and since
\alpha<1
, it holds that for all
s\in[0,t_{1}]

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\big(e^{-\frac{s}{\epsilon}}\partial_{v}Y(y,v,s)\big)=-e^{-\frac{s}{\epsilon}}\frac{\alpha V^{\alpha-1}\partial_{v}V}{\epsilon}\leq-e^{-\frac{s}{\epsilon}}\frac{\alpha v^{\alpha-1}}{2\epsilon}.

Thus, by integrating in time, we obtain that

\displaystyle e^{-\frac{s}{\epsilon}}\partial_{v}Y\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}e^{-\frac{s}{\epsilon}}v^{\alpha-1}-\frac{\alpha}{2}v^{\alpha-1},

for all
s\in[0,t_{1}],
and thus

\displaystyle\partial_{v}Y(y,v,s)\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}v^{\alpha-1}(1-e^{\frac{s}{\epsilon}})\leq 0.

Using similar arguments, we obtain that

\displaystyle\partial_{v}Y(y,v,s)\geq\frac{10^{1-\alpha}\alpha}{9^{1-\alpha}}v^{\alpha-1}(1-e^{\frac{s}{\epsilon}}).

(4.24)

By (
4.8
), we also have that for all
s\in[0,t_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\partial_{v}V(y,v,s)=-\frac{\gamma LV^{\gamma-1}\partial_{v}V}{1+|Y|^{d}}+\frac{dLV^{\gamma}|Y|^{d-2}Y\partial_{v}Y}{(1+|Y|^{d})^{2}},\qquad\partial_{v}V(y,v,0)=1.

(4.25)

We will prove that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\frac{V^{\gamma-1}(z)\textup{d}z}{1+|Y|^{d}(z)}\leq C\epsilon

(4.26)

and that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\frac{V^{\gamma}|Y|^{d-1}|\partial_{v}Y|}{(1+|Y|^{d})^{2}}\textup{d}z\leq C\epsilon,

(4.27)

for all
s\in[0,t_{1}]
. We first prove that (
4.26
) holds. By (
4.10
) we have that

\displaystyle\frac{V^{\gamma-1}}{1+|Y|^{d}}\leq\frac{v^{\gamma-1}}{1+|Y|^{d}}.

Moreover, we remember that

\displaystyle\partial_{s}Y(y,v,s)=\frac{Y}{\epsilon}-\frac{V^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}\leq\frac{v^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}\bigg[\frac{1}{3^{\alpha}}-\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}\bigg]\leq-\frac{cv^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}

and then as before we have the following cases.

i)

Y(y,v,0)=y\in[0,(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}]
and
Y(y,v,t_{1})<0
.

Then there exists
\tau:=\tau(y,v)\in[0,t_{1})
such that
Y(y,v,\tau(y,v))=0
and, for all
s\in[0,t_{1}]
, it holds that

\displaystyle|Y(y,v,s)|\geq\frac{cv^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}|s-\tau(y,v)|.

(4.28)

Then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\frac{V^{\gamma-1}(z)\textup{d}z}{1+|Y|^{d}(z)}\leq\int_{0}^{s}\frac{Cv^{\gamma-1}\textup{d}z}{1+\frac{v^{\alpha d}}{\epsilon^{d}}|z-\tau(y,v)|^{d}}.

We make the change of variable
\xi=\frac{v^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}(z-\tau)
and use the fact that we can consider without loss of generality that
v\geq 1
in order to obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\frac{V^{\gamma-1}(z)\textup{d}z}{1+|Y|^{d}(z)}\leq\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{C\epsilon v^{\gamma-\alpha-1}\textup{d}\xi}{1+|\xi|^{d}}\leq C\epsilon.

ii)

If
Y(y,v,0)=y\in[0,(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}]
and
Y(y,v,t_{1})\geq 0
, we have that

\displaystyle|Y(y,v,s)|=Y(y,v,s)\geq Y(y,v,s)-Y(y,v,t_{1})=|Y(y,v,s)-Y(y,v,t_{1})|\geq\frac{cv^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}|s-t_{1}|

and the result follows as before.

iii)

Otherwise
Y(y,v,0)=y<0
. Then for all
s\in[0,t_{1}]
, it holds by (
4.16
) that

\displaystyle Y(y,v,s)\leq-\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}\frac{v^{\alpha}s}{\epsilon}

and proceed as in the previous case in order to show that (
4.26
) holds.

We now prove (
4.27
). We only analyze the case when
Y(y,v,0)=y\in[0,(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}]
and
Y(y,v,t_{1})<0
as the other two cases can be treated in a similar, more straightforward manner.
Thus, let us assume
Y(y,v,0)=y\in[0,(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}]
and
Y(y,v,t_{1})<0
. Then, for every
y
and every
v>0
, there exists
\tau:=\tau(y,v)\in[0,t_{1})
such that
Y(y,v,\tau(y,v))=0
. We can write

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\frac{V^{\gamma}|Y|^{d-1}|\partial_{v}Y|}{(1+|Y|^{d})^{2}}\textup{d}z=\int_{0}^{\tau(y,v)}\frac{V^{\gamma}|Y|^{d-1}|\partial_{v}Y|}{(1+|Y|^{d})^{2}}\textup{d}z+\int_{\tau(y,v)}^{s}\frac{V^{\gamma}|Y|^{d-1}|\partial_{v}Y|}{(1+|Y|^{d})^{2}}\textup{d}z=:(I)+(II).

We bound
(II)
.
We have that

\displaystyle\partial_{s}Y=\frac{Y}{\epsilon}-\frac{V^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}\leq\frac{Y}{\epsilon}-(\frac{9}{10})^{\alpha}\frac{v^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}

and thus

\displaystyle\partial_{s}(e^{-\frac{s}{\epsilon}}Y)\leq-e^{-\frac{s}{\epsilon}}(\frac{9}{10})^{\alpha}\frac{v^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}.

We integrate from
\tau(y,v)
to
s\in[\tau(y,v),t_{1}]
and use the fact that
Y(y,v,\tau(y,v))=0
in order to obtain that

\displaystyle e^{-\frac{s}{\epsilon}}Y(s)\leq e^{-\frac{s}{\epsilon}}(\frac{9}{10})^{\alpha}v^{\alpha}-e^{-\frac{\tau}{\epsilon}}(\frac{9}{10})^{\alpha}v^{\alpha}

or in other words

\displaystyle Y(y,v,s)\leq(\frac{9}{10})^{\alpha}v^{\alpha}(1-e^{\frac{s-\tau}{\epsilon}})\leq 0.

(4.29)

Combining (
4.29
) with (
4.24
), we have that

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{V^{\gamma}|Y|^{d-1}\partial_{v}Y}{(1+|Y|^{d})^{2}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Cv^{\gamma}|\partial_{v}Y|}{1+|Y|^{d+1}}\leq\frac{Cv^{\gamma}|\partial_{v}Y|}{1+Cv^{\alpha(d+1)}(e^{\frac{s-\tau}{\epsilon}}-1)^{d+1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Cv^{\gamma+\alpha-1}(e^{\frac{s}{\epsilon}}-1)}{1+v^{\alpha(d+1)}(e^{\frac{s-\tau}{\epsilon}}-1)^{d+1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Cv^{\gamma-1}(e^{\frac{s}{\epsilon}}-1)}{1+v^{\alpha d}(e^{\frac{s-\tau}{\epsilon}}-1)^{d+1}}.

Thus

\displaystyle\int_{\tau}^{s}\bigg|\frac{V^{\gamma}|Y|^{d-2}Y\partial_{v}Y}{(1+|Y|^{d})^{2}}\bigg|\textup{d}z\leq\int_{\tau}^{s}\frac{Cv^{\gamma-1}(e^{\frac{z}{\epsilon}}-1)}{1+v^{\alpha d}(e^{\frac{z-\tau}{\epsilon}}-1)^{d+1}}\textup{d}z.

We make the change of variables
\xi=v^{\frac{d\alpha}{d+1}}(e^{\frac{z-\tau}{\epsilon}}-1)
and thus

\displaystyle\textup{d}\xi=\frac{v^{\frac{\alpha d}{d+1}}}{\epsilon}e^{\frac{z-\tau}{\epsilon}}\textup{d}z.

We have that
\tau\leq C\epsilon
. This is since if we choose
s=0
in (
4.28
), then
|y|\geq\frac{cv^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}|\tau(y,v)|
and we are in the case when
y\in[0,(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}]
. Using that
\tau\leq C\epsilon
and
v\geq 1
, we further obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{\tau}^{s}\bigg|\frac{V^{\gamma}|Y|^{d-2}Y\partial_{v}Y}{(1+|Y|^{d})^{2}}\bigg|\textup{d}z

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{C\epsilon v^{\gamma-\frac{\alpha d}{d+1}-1}e^{-\frac{z-\tau}{\epsilon}}(e^{\frac{z}{\epsilon}}-1)}{1+|\xi|^{d+1}}\textup{d}\xi\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{C\epsilon v^{\gamma-\frac{\alpha d}{d+1}-1}e^{\frac{\tau}{\epsilon}}(1-e^{-\frac{z}{\epsilon}})}{1+|\xi|^{d+1}}\textup{d}\xi

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{C\epsilon v^{\gamma-\frac{\alpha d}{d+1}-1}e^{\frac{\tau}{\epsilon}}}{1+|\xi|^{d+1}}\textup{d}\xi\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{C\epsilon v^{\gamma-\frac{\alpha d}{d+1}-1}}{1+|\xi|^{d+1}}\textup{d}\xi\leq C\epsilon

(4.30)

since we chose
d
sufficiently large such that
\gamma\leq\frac{d\alpha}{d+1}+1
.

We now bound
(I)
. For
s\leq\tau,
we only have that (
4.28
) holds. Using in addition (
4.24
), we have that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau(y,v)}\frac{V^{\gamma}|Y|^{d-1}|\partial_{v}Y|}{(1+|Y|^{d})^{2}}\textup{d}z\leq\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{Cv^{\gamma}|\partial_{v}Y|}{1+|Y|^{d+1}}\textup{d}z

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{Cv^{\gamma+\alpha-1}(e^{\frac{z}{\epsilon}}-1)}{1+\frac{v^{\alpha(d+1)}}{\epsilon^{d+1}}|z-\tau|^{d+1}}\textup{d}z

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{Cv^{\gamma-1}(e^{\frac{z}{\epsilon}}-1)}{1+\frac{v^{d\alpha}}{\epsilon^{d+1}}|z-\tau|^{d+1}}\textup{d}z.

We now make the change of variables

\displaystyle\xi

\displaystyle=\frac{v^{\frac{\alpha d}{d+1}}}{\epsilon}(\tau-z);

\displaystyle\textup{d}\xi

\displaystyle=-\frac{v^{\frac{\alpha d}{d+1}}}{\epsilon}\textup{d}z;

\displaystyle\frac{z}{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau-\epsilon v^{-\frac{\alpha d}{d+1}}\xi}{\epsilon}\leq\frac{\epsilon\xi+\tau}{\epsilon}=\xi+\frac{\tau}{\epsilon}\leq\xi+1.

Thus, using that
e^{x}-1\leq Cx,
when
x\in[0,1]
, we further obtain that

\displaystyle(I)\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{C\epsilon v^{\gamma-\frac{d\alpha}{d+1}-1}\frac{z}{\epsilon}}{1+|\xi|^{d+1}}\textup{d}\xi\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{C\epsilon v^{\gamma-\frac{d\alpha}{d+1}-1}(\xi+1)}{1+|\xi|^{d+1}}\textup{d}\xi\leq C\epsilon,

(4.31)

since
v\geq 1
and
\gamma\leq\frac{d\alpha}{d+1}+1
. Combining (
4.31
) with (
4.3
), we obtain that (
4.27
) holds.
We denote by

\displaystyle B(s):=\int_{0}^{s}\frac{L\gamma V^{\gamma-1}}{1+|Y|^{d}}\textup{d}z.

Notice that by (
4.26
), it holds that
0\leq B(s)\leq CL
, for all
s\in[0,t_{1}]
.
From (
4.25
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\big(e^{B(s)}\partial_{v}V(s)\big)\geq e^{B(s)}\frac{LdV^{\gamma}|Y|^{d-2}Y\partial_{v}Y}{(1+|Y|^{d})^{2}}\geq-e^{B(s)}\bigg|\frac{LdV^{\gamma}|Y|^{d-2}Y\partial_{v}Y}{(1+|Y|^{d})^{2}}\bigg|\geq-C(L)\bigg|\frac{V^{\gamma}|Y|^{d-2}Y\partial_{v}Y}{(1+|Y|^{d})^{2}}\bigg|,

for some constant
C(L)>0
which depends on
L
. Making use of (
4.27
), we further obtain that

\displaystyle e^{B(s)}\partial_{v}V(s)-1\geq-C(L)\epsilon

and since by (
4.26
) it holds that
0\leq B(s)\leq CL\epsilon
, for all
s\in[0,t_{1}]
, then there exists
\epsilon_{1}\in(0,1)
, which depends on
L
, such that for all
\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{1}
, the following holds

\displaystyle\partial_{v}V(s)\geq e^{-B(s)}(1-C(L)\epsilon)\geq e^{-CL\epsilon}(1-C(L)\epsilon)\geq\frac{3}{4},

for all
s\in[0,t_{1}]
. We can thus iterate the argument in order to obtain that for all times
t\geq 0
, we have that
\partial_{v}V(y,v,t)\geq\frac{1}{2}\geq 0
and
\partial_{v}Y(y,v,t)\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}v^{\alpha-1}(1-e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}})\leq 0
. This concludes our proof.
∎

Remark 4.8
.

As an immediate consequence, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{10^{1-\alpha}\alpha}{9^{1-\alpha}}v^{\alpha-1}(1-e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}})

\displaystyle\leq\partial_{v}Y(y,v,t)\leq\frac{\alpha v^{\alpha-1}}{2}(1-e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}});

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\leq\partial_{v}V(y,v,t)\leq 1;

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha v^{\alpha-1}}{2}e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha V^{\alpha-1}\partial_{v}{V}-\partial_{v}Y\leq\frac{10^{1-\alpha}\alpha}{9^{1-\alpha}}v^{\alpha-1}e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}.

Proposition 4.9
.

Let
L>0
. Let
G_{\epsilon,L}
be as in (
4.2
) and
\epsilon_{1}\in(0,1)
and
d>1
be as in Proposition
4.7
. It holds that there exists
K_{2}>0
, which is independent of
\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{1}
,
L>0
, but which can depend on
\alpha,\gamma,b,m
, such that for all
t\geq 0
and
\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{1}
, we have that

\displaystyle-\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon,L}(y,w,t)\leq-K_{2}\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon,L}(y,v,t),\textup{ for all }w\in\Big[\frac{v}{2},v\Big]\textup{ and all }y\leq\Big(\frac{v}{3}\Big)^{\alpha}.

Proof.

We remember that

\displaystyle\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon,L}(y,v,t)=-\frac{mAe^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(V^{\alpha}-Y)^{m-1}[\alpha V^{\alpha-1}\partial_{v}V-\partial_{v}Y]}{(1+V^{b})(1+(V^{\alpha}-Y)^{m})^{2}}-\frac{bAe^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}V^{b-1}\partial_{v}V}{(1+V^{b})^{2}(1+(V^{\alpha}-Y)^{m})},

with
A
as in (
2.1
). By Proposition
4.4
and Remark
4.6
, we have that

\displaystyle 0\leq\bigg[\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}-\frac{1}{3^{\alpha}}\bigg]e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(v^{\alpha}-y)\leq V^{\alpha}-Y\leq e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(v^{\alpha}-y).

(4.32)

By Remark
4.8
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha v^{\alpha-1}}{2}e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}\leq\alpha V^{\alpha-1}\partial_{v}{V}-\partial_{v}Y\leq\frac{10^{1-\alpha}\alpha}{9^{1-\alpha}}v^{\alpha-1}e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}.

(4.33)

By (
4.32
) and (
4.33
), we have that

\displaystyle\frac{me^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(V^{\alpha}-Y)^{m-1}[\alpha V^{\alpha-1}\partial_{v}V-\partial_{v}Y]}{(1+(V^{\alpha}-Y)^{m})^{2}}\geq C(\alpha,m)\frac{e^{\frac{t(m+1)}{\epsilon}}(v^{\alpha}-y)^{m-1}v^{\alpha-1}}{(1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}(v^{\alpha}-y)^{m})^{2}}.

It also holds that

\displaystyle\frac{be^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}V^{b-1}\partial_{v}V}{(1+V^{b})^{2}(1+(V^{\alpha}-Y)^{m})}\geq C(\alpha,b,m)\frac{e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}v^{b-1}}{(1+v^{b})^{2}(1+(v^{\alpha}-y)^{m})}.

Thus

\displaystyle-\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon,L}(y,v,t)\geq C(\alpha,b,m)A\bigg[\frac{e^{\frac{t(m+1)}{\epsilon}}(v^{\alpha}-y)^{m-1}v^{\alpha-1}}{(1+v^{b})(1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}(v^{\alpha}-y)^{m})^{2}}+\frac{e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}v^{b-1}}{(1+v^{b})^{2}(1+(v^{\alpha}-y)^{m})}\bigg].

We now look at the following system of ODE’s.

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}{Y}(y,w,t)&=-\frac{1}{\epsilon}({V}^{\alpha}-Y),&\qquad{Y}(y,w,0)&=y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}\,,\\
\partial_{t}{V}(y,w,t)&=-\frac{{L}{V}^{\gamma}}{1+|{Y}|^{d}},&\qquad{V}(y,w,0)&=w\in\Big[\frac{v}{2},v\Big].\end{aligned}\right.

(4.34)

We are able to reproduce the computations from Proposition
4.4
and Proposition
4.7
in order to obtain similar estimates for the solution
(\overline{Y},\overline{V})
of the system (
4.34
). This is since
y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}\leq(\frac{2}{3})^{\alpha}w^{\alpha}
. This implies that (
4.18
) becomes

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\overline{Y}\leq\frac{w^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}\bigg[\frac{2^{\alpha}}{3^{\alpha}}-\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}\bigg]\leq-\frac{\tilde{c}w^{\alpha}}{\epsilon}.

(4.35)

The rest of the proof follows similarly, but some constants need to be modified. We obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{9}{10}w

\displaystyle\leq\overline{V}(y,w,t)\leq w;

\displaystyle\overline{Y}(y,w,t)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{\alpha}}{3^{\alpha}}w^{\alpha};

\displaystyle e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-w^{\alpha})

\displaystyle\leq\overline{Y}-\overline{V}^{\alpha};

\displaystyle\overline{Y}-\overline{V}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg[\Big(\frac{9}{10}\Big)^{\alpha}-\frac{2^{\alpha}}{3^{\alpha}}\bigg]e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-w^{\alpha}).

The bounds for
\partial_{v}\overline{Y}(y,w,t)
and
\partial_{v}\overline{V}(y,w,t)
do not change. We thus obtain that

\displaystyle-\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon,L}(y,w,t)\leq C(\alpha,b,m)A\bigg[\frac{e^{\frac{t(m+1)}{\epsilon}}(w^{\alpha}-y)^{m-1}w^{\alpha-1}}{(1+w^{b})(1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}(w^{\alpha}-y)^{m})^{2}}+\frac{e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}w^{b-1}}{(1+w^{b})^{2}(1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}(w^{\alpha}-y)^{m})}\bigg].

If
y\leq 0
, we have that

\displaystyle v^{\alpha}-y\geq w^{\alpha}-y\geq\Big(\frac{v}{2}\Big)^{\alpha}-y\geq\frac{v^{\alpha}-y}{2^{\alpha}}.

If
y\in[0,(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}]
, then

\displaystyle v^{\alpha}-y\geq w^{\alpha}-y\geq\Big(\frac{v}{2}\Big)^{\alpha}-y\geq\frac{3^{\alpha}-2^{\alpha}}{6^{\alpha}}v^{\alpha}\geq\frac{3^{\alpha}-2^{\alpha}}{6^{\alpha}}(v^{\alpha}-y).

Concluding, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\frac{t(m+1)}{\epsilon}}(w^{\alpha}-y)^{m-1}w^{\alpha-1}}{(1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}(w^{\alpha}-y)^{m})^{2}}\leq C(\alpha,b,m)\frac{e^{\frac{t(m+1)}{\epsilon}}(v^{\alpha}-y)^{m-1}v^{\alpha-1}}{(1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}(v^{\alpha}-y)^{m})^{2}}

and further that

\displaystyle-\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon,L}(y,w,t)\leq-K_{2}\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon,L}(y,v,t).

∎

4.4
Proof of existence of a supersolution in the region
y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}

We are now in a position to prove the main statement of this section.

Proof of Proposition
4.1
.

Assume
H_{n}(y,v,t)\leq T_{1}(y,v,t)+T_{2}(y,v,t)+T_{3}(y,v,t)
. Let
C_{\gamma}:=1+\frac{1}{2^{\gamma}}
,
K_{0}
as in (
2.2
),
K_{1}
as in Proposition
4.3
,
K_{2}>0
be as in Proposition
4.9
, and
A
as in (
2.1
). Let

\displaystyle G_{\epsilon}:=e^{K_{3}(\alpha,b,m)Lt}G_{\epsilon,L},\textup{ where }G_{\epsilon,L}\textup{ solves }\eqref{equation supersolution}

(4.36)

with

\displaystyle L=C_{\gamma}K_{0}K_{1}K_{2}A^{3}.

(4.37)

Moreover,
d
is chosen as in (
4.7
) such that
d>1
, even, and sufficiently large such that
\gamma\leq\frac{d\alpha}{d+1}+1
, and
K_{3}(\alpha,b,m)
is a fixed constant depending on the parameters
\alpha,b,m
which will be determined later. Notice that
d
depends on
b
and the minimum value of
b
for which these conditions are satisfied determines the value of
\overline{b}(\gamma,\alpha)
in (
2.18
) as noticed in Remark
2.9
.

We prove Proposition
4.1
by first proving that
H_{n+1}(y,v,t)\leq G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t),
for all
t\in[0,T]
and
y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}
,
v\in(0,\infty),
and then that
G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\leq T_{3}(y,v,t)
for all
t\in[0,T]
and
y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}
,
v\in(0,\infty)
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. We prove that
G_{\epsilon}
is a supersolution for (
2.51
). In other words, we prove that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{y}[(v^{\alpha}-y)G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)]-

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{v}{2}}K(v-w,w)G_{\epsilon}(y,v-w,t)H_{n}(y,w,t)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\infty}K(v,w)G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)H_{n}(y,w,t)\textup{d}w\geq 0.

(4.38)

Since
K(v-w,w)\leq K(v,w),
for all
w\leq v
, it follows that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{y}[(v^{\alpha}-y)G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)]-

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{v}{2}}K(v-w,w)G_{\epsilon}(y,v-w,t)H_{n}(y,w,t)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\infty}K(v,w)G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)H_{n}(y,w,t)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\geq\partial_{t}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{y}[(v^{\alpha}-y)G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)]-

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{v}{2}}K(v-w,w)G_{\epsilon}(y,v-w,t)H_{n}(y,w,t)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\frac{v}{2}}K(v,w)G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)H_{n}(y,w,t)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\geq\partial_{t}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{y}[(v^{\alpha}-y)G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)]-

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{v}{2}}K(v,w)G_{\epsilon}(y,v-w,t)H_{n}(y,w,t)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\frac{v}{2}}K(v,w)G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)H_{n}(y,w,t)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{y}[(v^{\alpha}-y)G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)]-

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{v}{2}}K(v,w)[G_{\epsilon}(y,v-w,t)-G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)]H_{n}(y,w,t)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{y}[(v^{\alpha}-y)G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)]+

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{v}{2}}K(v,w)\int_{v-w}^{v}\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon}(y,z,t)\textup{d}zH_{n}(y,w,t)\textup{d}w=:(\ast).

From Proposition
4.4
, Proposition
4.7
, and Proposition
4.9
, we have that
G_{\epsilon}
has the following properties.

•

\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon}(y,z,t)\geq K_{2}\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)
, for all
z\in[v-w,v],

w\in[0,\frac{v}{2}];

•

\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\leq 0
, for all
y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}
.

Since also
K(v,w)\leq C_{\gamma}K_{0}v^{\gamma}
by (
2.2
) when
w\in[0,\frac{v}{2}]
, we obtain that

\displaystyle(\ast)\geq\partial_{t}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{y}[(v^{\alpha}-y)G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)]+

\displaystyle C_{\gamma}K_{0}K_{2}v^{\gamma}\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\int_{0}^{\frac{v}{2}}wH_{n}(y,w,t)\textup{d}w.

(4.39)

Making use of Proposition
4.3
, the non-positivity of
\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon}
in (
4.39
), and since by assumption
H_{n}\leq T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle(\ast)

\displaystyle\geq\partial_{t}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{y}[(v^{\alpha}-y)G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)]

\displaystyle+C_{\gamma}K_{0}K_{2}v^{\gamma}\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\int_{0}^{\frac{v}{2}}w[T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}](y,w,t)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\geq\partial_{t}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{y}[(v^{\alpha}-y)G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)]+\frac{C_{\gamma}K_{0}K_{1}K_{2}A^{3}v^{\gamma}}{1+|y|^{\frac{b-2}{\alpha}}}\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)

\displaystyle\geq\partial_{t}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}(v^{\alpha}-y)\partial_{y}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+\frac{Lv^{\gamma}}{1+|y|^{d}}\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)-\frac{1}{\epsilon}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t),

where in the last line we used that
\frac{b-2}{\alpha}\geq d
. Notice that this is equation (
4.2
) when
v\geq 1
and thus
G_{\epsilon}
is a supersolution for (
2.51
). Thus

\displaystyle(\ast)

\displaystyle\geq\partial_{t}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}(v^{\alpha}-y)\partial_{y}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+\frac{L\xi_{R}(v)v^{\gamma}}{1+|y|^{d}}\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)-\frac{1}{\epsilon}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)

\displaystyle-\bigg|\frac{L[1-\xi_{R}](v)v^{\gamma}}{1+|y|^{d}}\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\bigg|

\displaystyle\geq\partial_{t}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}(v^{\alpha}-y)\partial_{y}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+\frac{L\xi_{R}(v)v^{\gamma}}{1+|y|^{d}}\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)-\frac{1}{\epsilon}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)

\displaystyle-\bigg|\frac{L\mathbbm{1}_{[0,1]}(v)v^{\gamma}}{1+|y|^{d}}\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\bigg|.

Assume that

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{L\mathbbm{1}_{[0,1]}(v)v^{\gamma}}{1+|y|^{d}}\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\bigg|\leq K_{3}(\alpha,b,m)LG_{\epsilon}(y,v,t).

(4.40)

Then

\displaystyle(\ast)\geq

\displaystyle\partial_{t}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}(v^{\alpha}-y)\partial_{y}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+\frac{L\xi_{R}(v)v^{\gamma}}{1+|y|^{d}}\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)-\frac{1}{\epsilon}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)

\displaystyle-K_{3}(\alpha,b,m)LG_{\epsilon}(y,v,t).

By (
4.36
) , it holds that
\partial_{t}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)=e^{K_{3}(\alpha,b,m)Lt}\partial_{t}G_{\epsilon,L}(y,v,t)+K_{3}(\alpha,b,m)LG_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)
. Thus

\displaystyle(\ast)\geq

\displaystyle e^{K_{3}Lt}\bigg[\partial_{t}G_{\epsilon,L}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}(v^{\alpha}-y)\partial_{y}G_{\epsilon,L}(y,v,t)+\frac{L\xi_{R}(v)v^{\gamma}}{1+|y|^{d}}\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon,L}(y,v,t)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\epsilon}G_{\epsilon,L}(y,v,t)\bigg]=0.

This concludes our proof.

It remains to prove that (
4.40
) holds. We have proved in Proposition
4.9
that

\displaystyle-\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon,L}(y,v,t)\leq C(m,\alpha,b)A\bigg[\frac{e^{\frac{t(m+1)}{\epsilon}}(v^{\alpha}-y)^{m-1}v^{\alpha-1}}{(1+v^{b})(1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}(v^{\alpha}-y)^{m})^{2}}+\frac{e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}v^{b-1}}{(1+v^{b})^{2}(1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}(v^{\alpha}-y)^{m})}\bigg].

Thus

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{L\mathbbm{1}_{[0,1]}(v)v^{\gamma}}{1+|y|^{d}}\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\bigg|\leq C(\alpha,b,m)Lv^{\gamma}\mathbbm{1}_{[0,1]}(v)G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\bigg[\frac{e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}(v^{\alpha}-y)^{m-1}v^{\alpha-1}}{1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}(v^{\alpha}-y)^{m}}+\frac{v^{b-1}}{1+v^{b}}\bigg].

Since
y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}
and thus
v^{\alpha}-y\geq C(\alpha)v^{\alpha}
, we further have that

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{L\mathbbm{1}_{[0,1]}(v)v^{\gamma}}{1+|y|^{d}}\partial_{v}G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha,b,m)Lv^{\gamma-1}\mathbbm{1}_{[0,1]}(v)G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\bigg[\frac{e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}(v^{\alpha}-y)^{m}}{1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}(v^{\alpha}-y)^{m}}+\frac{v^{b}}{1+v^{b}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha,b,m)Lv^{\gamma-1}\mathbbm{1}_{[0,1]}(v)G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\leq C(\alpha,b,m)LG_{\epsilon}(y,v,t),

(4.41)

since
\gamma>1
and thus (
4.40
) holds if we choose
K_{3}
as above. Thus, we have that
H_{n+1}\leq G_{\epsilon}
when
y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}
.

We now prove that
G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\leq T_{3}(y,v,t)
for all
t\in[0,T]
and
y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}
,
v\in(0,\infty)
. Let
y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}
. If

\displaystyle t\leq\frac{1}{K_{3}(\alpha,b,m)L},

(4.42)

where
K_{3}(\alpha,b,m)
is as in (
4.41
) and
L
as in (
4.37
), then
e^{K_{3}(\alpha,b,m)Lt}\leq 3
. From Proposition
4.4
, we have that

\displaystyle(1+v^{b})G_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(\alpha,b,m)e^{K_{3}(\alpha,b,m)Lt}Ae^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}}{1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}|v^{\alpha}-y|^{m}}\leq\frac{3C(\alpha,b,m)Ae^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}}{1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}|v^{\alpha}-y|^{m}}

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{2}Ae^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}}{1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}|v^{\alpha}-y|^{m}}=T_{3}(y,v,t)

(4.43)

by the definition of
T_{3}
is (
2.13
). We also remark that the constant
M_{2}
in the above inequality depends only on the choice of parameters
\alpha,b,m
since it appears due to the bounds found in Proposition
4.4
and does not depend on
n
. This concludes our proof.
∎

5
Proof of Theorem
2.7
- Existence

This section is dedicated to the proof of Theorem

2.7
. We remember the following.

Let
b\geq\operatorname{max}\{\overline{b}(\gamma,\alpha),\gamma+1\}
and
m>\operatorname{max}\{\frac{2(\gamma+1)}{\alpha},\frac{b}{\alpha}+1\}
, where
\overline{b}(\gamma,\alpha)
is as in (
2.18
). By Proposition
3.3
, there exists
\epsilon_{1}\in(0,1)
and
t\in[0,1]
sufficiently small such that for all
\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{1}
, such that
H_{n}(y,v,s)\leq T_{1}(s)+T_{2}(s),
for all
s\in[0,t]
,
y\geq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}
, and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. By Proposition
4.1
, there exist
\epsilon_{1}\in(0,1)
and
T\leq 1
sufficiently small such that for all
\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{1}
,
H_{n}(y,v,t)\leq T_{3}(y,v,t),
for all
t\in[0,T]
,
y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}
,
v\in(0,\infty)
, and for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
. In other words, we have that

\displaystyle H_{n}(y,v,t)\leq T_{1}(y,v,t)+T_{2}(y,v,t)+T_{3}(y,v,t),\textup{ for all }n\in\mathbb{N},

(5.1)

for all
t\in[0,T]
, with
T\leq 1
, sufficiently small, and all
y\in\mathbb{R}
,
v\in(0,\infty)
. We will prove that

Claim:
Assume in addition that
b>2\gamma+1
. For all
n\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T],y\in\mathbb{R},v\in(0,\infty)}|H_{n+1}(y,v,t)-H_{n}(y,v,t)|\leq(CT)^{n}\rightarrow 0\textup{ as }n\rightarrow 0\textup{ for }T<\frac{1}{2C}.

(5.2)

A similar strategy can then be employed in order to prove that for all
n,\tilde{n}\in\mathbb{N}
,
\tilde{n}\geq n+1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T],y\in\mathbb{R},v\in(0,\infty)}|H_{\tilde{n}}(y,v,t)-H_{n}(y,v,t)|\leq(CT)^{n}\rightarrow 0\textup{ as }n\rightarrow 0\textup{ for }T<\frac{1}{2C}.

Assuming that (
5.2
) is true, we have that for fixed
\epsilon
there exists a limit
H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\in\textup{C}([0,T];L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))
such that
H_{n}\rightarrow H_{\epsilon}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
and moreover

\displaystyle H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\leq T_{1}(y,v,t)+T_{2}(y,v,t)+T_{3}(y,v,t).

Thus, assuming (
5.2
) holds, what is left to prove is that
H_{\epsilon}
solves (
2.4
). We have that
H_{n}
satisfies

\displaystyle H_{n+1}(y,v,t)-S_{\epsilon}(t)[H_{n+1}(y,v,0)]D[H_{n}](y,v,0,t)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{(0,v)}D[H_{n}](y,v,s,t)S_{\epsilon}(t-s)\bigg[K(v-v^{\prime},v^{\prime})H_{n+1}(y,v-w,s)H_{n}(y,w,s)\bigg]\textup{d}s

and wish to pass to the limit in the equation. Notice that

\displaystyle H_{n}(e^{\frac{t-s}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},w,s)\leq[T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}](e^{\frac{t-s}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},w,s),

(5.3)

where
T_{1},T_{2},T_{3}
are as in (
2.11
)-(
2.13
). Denote by

\displaystyle X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,s):=e^{\frac{t-s}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha}.

We can then replace
y
by
X_{\epsilon}
in the proof of Proposition
4.3
in order to obtain the following.

Proposition 5.1

(Moment estimates)
.

Let
b>\gamma+1
and
m\geq\frac{b}{\alpha}+1
in (
2.11
)-(
2.13
). Let
T\leq 1
and
H_{n}
be as in (
5.3
). It then holds that

\displaystyle\int_{(0,\infty)}

\displaystyle w^{k}H_{n}(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,s),w,s)\textup{d}w\leq\frac{K_{1}A^{3}}{1+|X_{\epsilon}|^{d}},\textup{ for some }d\in\mathbb{N}

(5.4)

and all
k\in[0,\gamma+1]
,
t\in[0,T],s\in[0,t],y\in\mathbb{R},v\in(0,\infty).

Proposition 5.2
.

H_{\epsilon}
solves (
2.4
).

Proof.

We prove first that

\displaystyle D[H_{n}](y,v,s,t)\rightarrow D[H_{\epsilon}](y,v,s,t).

Using
|e^{-x}-e^{-y}|\leq|x-y|,
for
x,y\geq 0
, we have that

\displaystyle\bigg|

\displaystyle\textup{e}^{-\int_{s}^{t}a[H_{n}](e^{\frac{t-\tau}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},v,\tau)\textup{d}\tau}-\textup{e}^{-\int_{s}^{t}a[H_{\epsilon}](e^{\frac{t-\tau}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},v,\tau)\textup{d}\tau}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\int_{s}^{t}a[H_{n}](e^{\frac{t-\tau}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},v,\tau)\textup{d}\tau-\int_{s}^{t}a[H_{\epsilon}](e^{\frac{t-\tau}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},v,\tau)\textup{d}\tau\bigg|,

where
a[H_{\epsilon}]
is as in (
2.6
). However

\displaystyle a[H_{n}](e^{\frac{t-\tau}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},v,\tau)=\int_{(0,\infty)}K(v,w)H_{n}(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,\tau),w,\tau)\textup{d}w.

Let
M>1
. Since
H_{n}\rightarrow H_{\epsilon}
as
\epsilon\rightarrow 0,
we have that

\displaystyle\int_{(0,M)}K(v,w)H_{n}(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,\tau),w,\tau)\textup{d}w\rightarrow\int_{(0,M)}K(v,w)H_{\epsilon}(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,\tau),w,\tau)\textup{d}w

as
n\rightarrow\infty
, while by Proposition
5.1
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{(M,\infty)}K(v,w)H_{n}(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,\tau),w,\tau)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\leq M^{-1}\int(v^{\gamma}w+w^{\gamma+1})H_{n}(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,\tau),w,\tau)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\leq CM^{-1}(1+v^{\gamma})\rightarrow 0\textup{ as }M\rightarrow\infty.

The rest of the proof follows the same idea. For example, we have that

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{(0,v)}

\displaystyle S_{\epsilon}(t-s)\bigg[K(v-w,w)H_{n+1}(y,v-w,s)H_{n}(y,w,s)\bigg]\textup{d}s

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{(0,v)}S_{\epsilon}(t-s)\bigg[K(v-w,w)H_{\epsilon}(y,v-w,s)H_{\epsilon}(y,w,s)\bigg]\textup{d}s\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\int_{(0,v)}S_{\epsilon}(t-s)\bigg[K(v-w,w)|H_{n+1}-H_{\epsilon}|(y,v-w,s)H_{n}(y,w,s)\bigg]\textup{d}s

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{(0,v)}S_{\epsilon}(t-s)\bigg[K(v-w,w)H_{\epsilon}(y,v-w,s)|H_{n}-H_{\epsilon}|(y,w,s)\bigg]\textup{d}s.

We can use the convergence of
H_{n}
to
H_{\epsilon}
together with the moment estimates from Proposition
5.1
in order to conclude as above. More precisely, by Proposition
5.1
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{(0,v)}S_{\epsilon}(t-s)\bigg[K(v-w,w)|H_{n+1}-H_{\epsilon}|(y,v-w,s)H_{n}(y,w,s)\bigg]\textup{d}s

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{(0,v)}e^{\frac{t-s}{\epsilon}}K(v-w,w)|H_{n+1}-H_{\epsilon}|(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,s),v-w,s)H_{n}(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,s),w,s)\textup{d}w\textup{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(\epsilon,v)||H_{n+1}-H_{\epsilon}||_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{(0,v)}H_{n}(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,s),w,s)\textup{d}w\textup{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(\epsilon,v)||H_{n+1}-H_{\epsilon}||_{L^{\infty}}\rightarrow 0\textup{ as }n\rightarrow\infty.

The rest of the steps are done in the same manner using in addition that

\displaystyle(1+v^{b})H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\leq Ce^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}.

This concludes our proof.
∎

The rest of this subsection is dedicated to proving that (
5.2
) is true.

Proof of (
5.2
).

Step 1: (Set-up)

Let
t\geq 0
,
y\in\mathbb{R}
and
v>0
. For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote by

\displaystyle R_{n}(y,v,t):=H_{n+1}(y,v,t)-H_{n}(y,v,t).

(5.5)

For two functions
f,g
, we denote by

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{1}[f,g]

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\frac{v}{2}}e^{\frac{t-s}{\epsilon}}K(v-w,w)f(e^{\frac{t-s}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},w,s)g(e^{\frac{t-s}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},v-w,s)\textup{d}w\textup{d}s,

(5.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{1}[f,g]

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{\frac{v}{2}}K(v-w,w)f(y,w,t)g(y,v-w,t)\textup{d}w,

(5.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{2}[f,g]

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\frac{t-s}{\epsilon}}K(v,w)f(e^{\frac{t-s}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},w,s)g(e^{\frac{t-s}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},v,s)\textup{d}w\textup{d}s,

(5.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{2}[f,g]

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{\infty}K(v,w)f(y,w,t)g(y,v,t)\textup{d}w,

(5.9)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[f,g]:=\mathcal{C}_{1}[f,g]-\mathcal{C}_{2}[f,g],

(5.10)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}[f,g]:=\mathcal{K}_{1}[f,g]-\mathcal{K}_{2}[f,g].

(5.11)

Using this notation, it holds that

\displaystyle R_{n}(y,v,t)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}[H_{n},R_{n}]+\mathcal{C}[R_{n-1},H_{n}].

(5.12)

We notice that
\mathcal{C}[H_{n},R_{n}]
is linear in
H_{n}
, while
\mathcal{C}[R_{n-1},H_{n}]
does not depend on
H_{n}
. We can thus use Duhamel’s principle in order to deduce that in order to find estimates for
R_{n}
which solves

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}R_{n}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{y}[(v^{\alpha}-y)R_{n}(y,v,t)]&=\mathcal{K}[H_{n},R_{n}]+\mathcal{K}[R_{n-1},H_{n}];\\
R_{n}(y,v,0)&=0\end{aligned}\right.

(5.13)

it is sufficient to find estimates for the system

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}R^{s}_{n}(x,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{y}[(v^{\alpha}-y)R_{n}(y,v,t)]&=\mathcal{K}[H_{n},R_{n}],\textup{ for }t>s;\\
R^{s}_{n}(y,v,s)&=\mathcal{K}[R_{n-1},H_{n}].\end{aligned}\right.

(5.14)

Step 2: (Bound for inhomogeneous term)

We wish to bound
\mathcal{C}[R_{n-1},H_{n}]
by induction. We only bound from above
\mathcal{C}_{2}[R_{n-1},H_{n}]
as
\mathcal{C}_{1}[R_{n-1},H_{n}]
is bounded using similar computations.

Induction basis:
By Proposition
5.1
, (
2.53
)-(
2.54
), and since
(1+v^{b})|H_{n}(y,v,t)|\leq Ce^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}
, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{2}[H_{0},H_{1}]\leq

\displaystyle(1+v^{\gamma})\int_{0}^{t}e^{\frac{t-s}{\epsilon}}H_{1}(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,s),v,s)\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+w^{\gamma})|H_{0}|(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,s),w,s)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\leq(1+v^{\gamma})\int_{0}^{t}e^{\frac{t-s}{\epsilon}}H_{1}(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,s),v,s)\frac{K_{1}A^{3}}{1+|X|^{d}}\leq Cte^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}\leq Cte^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}},

where
C
does not depend on
n
or
\epsilon
.

Induction step:
Assume by induction that

\displaystyle|R_{n-1}|(y,v,t)\leq(Cte^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}})^{n-1}\frac{1+v^{\gamma}}{1+v^{b}},

where
C
does not depend on
n
or on
\epsilon
. Then, since
(1+v^{b})|H_{n}(y,v,t)|\leq Ce^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}
and since
b>2\gamma+1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{2}[R_{n-1},H_{n}]\leq

\displaystyle(1+v^{\gamma})\int_{0}^{t}e^{\frac{t-s}{\epsilon}}H_{n}(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,s),v,s)\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+w^{\gamma})|R_{n-1}|(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,s),w,s)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\leq(1+v^{\gamma})\int_{0}^{t}e^{\frac{t-s}{\epsilon}}H_{n}(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,s),v,s)\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1+w^{2\gamma}}{1+w^{b}}\textup{d}w(Cse^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}})^{n-1}\textup{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}e^{\frac{t-s}{\epsilon}}(1+v^{\gamma})H_{n}(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,s),v,s)(Cse^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}})^{n-1}\textup{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C^{n}t^{n}e^{\frac{n-1}{\epsilon}}e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}\frac{1+v^{\gamma}}{1+v^{b}}\leq(Cte^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}})^{n}\frac{1+v^{\gamma}}{1+v^{b}}.

(5.15)

Step 3: (Iteration in time in strong formulation)

By Step
2
, we deduce that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, it holds that

\displaystyle R_{n}(y,v,t)\leq(Cte^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}})^{n}.

(5.16)

Thus, for
t<\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{C}
, we have that

\displaystyle R_{n}(y,v,t)\rightarrow 0\textup{ as }n\rightarrow\infty.

(5.17)

A similar argument has been used in
[
9
]
.

We now wish to extend (
5.17
) to hold for times of order
\mathcal{O}(1)
. Let us assume for simplicity of notation that
C=1
in (
5.16
). The idea is to iterate the argument in Step
2.
to hold for longer times. For simplicity, we first show the argument using the strong form of
(
2.61
). In order to obtain a rigorous proof, the same idea needs to be applied directly to the mild formulation (
2.61
). This will be done in Step
4.

Translation in time:
We first do a translation in time. For
t\in[0,\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}]
, define

\displaystyle\tilde{R}_{n}(t):=R_{n}\bigg(t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg).

Notice that
\tilde{R}_{n}(t):=R_{n}\bigg(t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)=H_{n+1}\bigg(t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)-H_{n}\bigg(t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)
and thus

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}\tilde{R}_{n}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{y}[(v^{\alpha}-y)\tilde{R}_{n}(y,v,t)]&=\mathcal{K}\bigg[H_{n}\bigg(t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg),\tilde{R}_{n}\bigg]+\mathcal{K}\bigg[\tilde{R}_{n-1},H_{n}\bigg(t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)\bigg];\\
\tilde{R}_{n}(y,v,0)&=H_{n+1}\bigg(\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)-H_{n}\bigg(\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg).\end{aligned}\right.

(5.18)

Initial condition becomes zero:
Let now

\displaystyle P_{n}(y,v,t)

\displaystyle:=\tilde{R}_{n}(y,v,t)-e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}R_{n}\bigg(e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},v,\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg);

\displaystyle\tilde{P}_{n}(y,v,t)

\displaystyle:=e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}R_{n}\bigg(e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},v,\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg).

Then
P_{n}
satisfies

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}P_{n}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{y}[(v^{\alpha}-y)P_{n}(y,v,t)]&=\mathcal{K}\bigg[H_{n}\bigg(t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg),P_{n}\bigg]+\mathcal{K}\bigg[P_{n-1},H_{n}\bigg(t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)\bigg]\\
&+\mathcal{K}\bigg[H_{n}\bigg(t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg),\tilde{P}_{n}\bigg]+\mathcal{K}\bigg[\tilde{P}_{n-1},H_{n}\bigg(t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)\bigg]\\
P(y,v,0)&=0.\end{aligned}\right.

(5.19)

By Duhamel’s principle, this becomes equivalent to solving

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}P_{n}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{y}[(v^{\alpha}-y)P_{n}(y,v,t)]&=\mathcal{K}\bigg[H_{n}\bigg(t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg),P_{n}\bigg]\textup{ for }t>s\\
P_{n}(y,v,s)&=\mathcal{K}\bigg[P_{n-1},H_{n}\bigg(s+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)\bigg]+\mathcal{K}\bigg[H_{n}\bigg(s+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg),\tilde{P}_{n}\bigg]\\
&+\mathcal{K}\bigg[\tilde{P}_{n-1},H_{n}\bigg(s+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)\bigg]=:I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.\end{aligned}\right.

(5.20)

We are now able to reproduce the estimates we performed in Step
2
in order to obtain that there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle P_{n}(t)\leq(Cte^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}})^{n}+2^{-n}\textup{ for all }n\in\mathbb{N}.

(5.21)

Estimates for the iteration in time:
The term
I_{1}
we bound by induction as in Step
2
, (
5
), using in addition that

\displaystyle P_{n}(t)=

\displaystyle R_{n}\bigg(y,v,t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)-e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}R_{n}\bigg(\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)\leq R_{n}\bigg(y,v,t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq H_{n+1}\bigg(y,v,t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)+H_{n}\bigg(y,v,t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg).

I_{2}
and
I_{3}
can then bounded in a similar manner. We thus only show
I_{2}
. More precisely, we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{2}

\displaystyle\bigg[H_{n}\bigg(t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg),\tilde{P}_{n}\bigg]

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\frac{t-s}{\epsilon}}K(v,w)H_{n}(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,s),w,s+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2})e^{\frac{s}{\epsilon}}R_{n}\bigg(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,0),v,\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)\textup{d}w\textup{d}s.

By the upper bound in Theorem
2.7
, we have that
(1+v^{b})|H_{n}(y,v,s)|\leq Ce^{\frac{s}{\epsilon}}
, for all
t\leq 1
. Moreover, by Step
2
it holds that
R_{n}(t)\leq(te^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}})^{n}\frac{1+v^{\gamma}}{1+v^{b}}
. Thus, we have that
R_{n}\big(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,0),v,\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\big)\leq\frac{1}{2^{n}}\frac{1+v^{\gamma}}{1+v^{b}}
.This implies that as long as
2t\leq 1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{2}

\displaystyle\bigg[H_{n}\bigg(t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg),\tilde{P}_{n}\bigg]\leq\frac{1}{2^{n}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\frac{t+s+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}}{\epsilon}}\textup{d}s\leq e^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{te^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2^{n}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{e}}{2^{n+1}}.

We bound
I_{3}
similarly and conclude that (
5.21
) holds. Since

\displaystyle R_{n}\bigg(y,v,t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)=P_{n}(t)+e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}R_{n}\bigg(X_{\epsilon}(y,v,t,0),v,\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)

we then obtain that

\displaystyle R_{n}\bigg(y,v,t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)\leq e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(Ct)^{n}+2^{-n+1}\rightarrow 0\textup{ as }n\rightarrow\infty,\textup{ for all }t\leq\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}.

We can then iterate Step
3
to extend the result to hold for all times as long as
t\leq\operatorname{min}\{2^{-1},T\}
with
T
as in Theorem
2.7
.

Step 4: (Iteration in time in mild formulation)

As mentioned in Step
3,
we now show the translation in time argument directly for the mild formulation (
2.61
).

Mild formulation. Translation in time

Since
R_{n}(y,v,0)=0,

R_{n}
satisfies the following mild formulation

\displaystyle R_{n}(y,v,t)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}[H_{n},R_{n}]+\mathcal{C}[R_{n-1},H_{n}].

Define now

\displaystyle\tilde{R}_{n}(t):=R_{n}\bigg(t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg).

Notice as before that
\tilde{R}_{n}(t):=R_{n}\bigg(t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)=H_{n+1}\bigg(t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)-H_{n}\bigg(t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)
. We show the argument for
\mathcal{C}_{2}[H_{n},R_{n}]
in (
5.9
) as the other terms are obtained in the same manner.

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{2}[H_{n},R_{n}]\bigg(t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\frac{t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}-s}{\epsilon}}K(v,w)H_{n}(e^{\frac{t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}-s}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},w,s)R_{n}(e^{\frac{t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}-s}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},v,s)\textup{d}w\textup{d}s

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\frac{t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}-s}{\epsilon}}K(v,w)H_{n}(e^{\frac{t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}-s}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},w,s)R_{n}(e^{\frac{t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}-s}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},v,s)\textup{d}w\textup{d}s

\displaystyle+\int_{\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}}^{t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\frac{t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}-s}{\epsilon}}K(v,w)H_{n}(e^{\frac{t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}-s}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},w,s)R_{n}(e^{\frac{t+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}-s}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},v,s)\textup{d}w\textup{d}s

\displaystyle:=L_{1}+L_{2}.

For
L_{2}
we make the following change of variable
\tilde{s}=s-\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}
. It thus holds that

\displaystyle L_{2}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\frac{t-\tilde{s}}{\epsilon}}K(v,w)H_{n}\bigg(e^{\frac{t-\tilde{s}}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},w,\tilde{s}+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)R_{n}\bigg(e^{\frac{t-\tilde{s}}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},v,\tilde{s}+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)\textup{d}w\textup{d}\tilde{s}.

Thus,
\tilde{R}_{n}
satisfies the following equation.

\displaystyle\tilde{R}_{n}(y,v,t)=e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}R_{n}\bigg(e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},v,\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)+\mathcal{C}\bigg[H_{n}\bigg(\cdot+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg),\tilde{R}_{n}\bigg]+\mathcal{C}\bigg[\tilde{R}_{n-1},H_{n}\bigg(\cdot+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)\bigg].

Mild formulation. Initial condition becomes
0
:
Lastly, let

\displaystyle P_{n}(y,v,t)

\displaystyle:=\tilde{R}_{n}(y,v,t)-e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}R_{n}\bigg(e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},v,\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\tilde{P}_{n}(y,v,t)

\displaystyle:=e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}R_{n}\bigg(e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})+v^{\alpha},v,\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg).

Then
P_{n}
satisfies

\displaystyle\tilde{P}_{n}(y,v,t)=\mathcal{C}\bigg[H_{n}\bigg(\cdot+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg),\tilde{R}_{n}\bigg]+\mathcal{C}\bigg[\tilde{R}_{n-1},H_{n}\bigg(\cdot+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)\bigg].

In other words,
P_{n}
satisfies

\left\{\begin{aligned} P_{n}(y,v,t)&=\mathcal{C}\bigg[H_{n}\bigg(\cdot+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg),\tilde{R}_{n}\bigg]+\mathcal{C}\bigg[\tilde{R}_{n-1},H_{n}\bigg(\cdot+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)\bigg]\\
&+\mathcal{C}\bigg[H_{n}\bigg(\cdot+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg),\tilde{P}_{n}\bigg]+\mathcal{C}\bigg[\tilde{P}_{n-1},H_{n}\bigg(\cdot+\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}{2}\bigg)\bigg]\\
P(y,v,0)&=0.\end{aligned}\right.

(5.22)

We are now able to reproduce the estimates from Step
3.
This concludes our proof.∎

6
Proof of Theorem
2.12
- Convergence to a Dirac measure

Proposition 6.1

(Moment estimates - general version)
.

Let
b>\gamma+3
and
m>\gamma+3-\alpha
. Let
T\leq 1
. There exist
r>0
and
\omega:=\operatorname{min}\{m-\gamma-1+\alpha,b-\gamma-1\}>2
such that

\displaystyle\int_{(0,\infty)}(w^{-r}+w^{\gamma})H_{\epsilon}(y,w,t)\textup{d}w\leq\frac{C}{1+|y|^{\omega}},\textup{ for all }t\in[0,T],y\in\mathbb{R}.

(6.1)

and thus

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}(1+|y|)H_{\epsilon}(y,w,t)\textup{d}w\textup{d}y\leq C,\textup{ for all }t\in[0,T].

(6.2)

Proof.

Since
\alpha<1
, there exists
r\in(0,1)
such that
\alpha+2r\leq 1
.

(
6.1
) is a more general version of the proof of Proposition
4.3
. We only have to prove that
\int_{(0,\infty)}w^{-r}H_{\epsilon}(y,w,t)\textup{d}w\leq\frac{C}{1+|y|^{\omega}}
. Let
C_{0}
be as in Proposition
3.3
. Let
y\geq 0
. We distinguish between different regions.

•

Assume first that
\{y\geq w^{\alpha}-C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}\}
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{\{y\geq w^{\alpha}-C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}\}}w^{-r}P(y,w,t)\textup{d}w\leq\int_{(0,\infty)}\frac{Cw^{-r}e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}}{(1+w^{b})(1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}|y-w^{\alpha}|^{m})}\textup{d}w.

We make the change of variables

z=e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-w^{\alpha})
and we obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{\{y\geq w^{\alpha}-C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}\}}w^{-r}P(y,w,t)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\leq\int_{(-\infty,e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}y)}\frac{C(y-e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}z)^{\frac{1-\alpha-r}{\alpha}}}{(1+(y-e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}z)^{\frac{b}{\alpha}})(1+|z|^{m})}\textup{d}z.

On one side, if
|z|\leq\frac{e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}y}{2}
, it holds that
\frac{y}{2}\leq y-e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}z\leq\frac{3y}{2}
. Thus

\displaystyle\int_{|z|\leq\frac{e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}y}{2}}\frac{C(y-e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}z)^{\frac{1-\alpha-r}{\alpha}}}{(1+(y-e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}z)^{\frac{b}{\alpha}})(1+|z|^{m})}\textup{d}z

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Cy^{\frac{1-\alpha-r}{\alpha}}}{1+|y|^{\frac{b}{\alpha}}}\int_{(-\infty,\infty)}\frac{C}{1+|z|^{m}}\leq\frac{C}{1+|y|^{\frac{b+r-1+\alpha}{\alpha}}}.

(6.3)

On the other hand, since
1-\alpha-r\geq 0
, it holds that
\frac{(y-e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}z)^{\frac{1-\alpha-r}{\alpha}}}{(1+(y-e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}z)^{\frac{b}{\alpha}})}\leq C
. Thus

\displaystyle\int_{|z|\geq\frac{e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}y}{2}}\frac{C(y-e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}z)^{\frac{1-\alpha-r}{\alpha}}}{(1+(y-e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}z)^{\frac{b}{\alpha}})(1+|z|^{m})}\textup{d}z

\displaystyle\leq\int_{|z|\geq\frac{e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}y}{2}}\frac{C}{1+|z|^{m}}\textup{d}z\leq\frac{C}{1+(e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}y)^{m-1}}\leq\frac{C}{1+y^{m-1}}.

(6.4)

Combining (
6.3
) and (
6.4
), we obtain that (
6.1
) holds in the region
\{y\geq w^{\alpha}-C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}\}
.

•

We now analyze the region
(\frac{w}{3})^{\alpha}\leq y\leq w^{\alpha}-C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}
. Notice that in this region it holds that
y^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\leq(y+C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}})^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\leq w\leq 3y^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}
. Thus

\displaystyle\int_{\{(\frac{w}{3})^{\alpha}\leq y\leq w^{\alpha}-C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}\}}w^{-r}P(y,w,t)\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\leq\int^{3y^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}}_{(y+C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}})^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}}\frac{Cw^{-r}\epsilon}{(1+w^{b})|y-w^{\alpha}|}\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\epsilon y^{\frac{-r}{\alpha}}}{1+y^{\frac{b}{\alpha}}}\int^{3y^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}}_{(y+C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}})^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}}\frac{1}{|y-w^{\alpha}|}\textup{d}w.

We make the change of variables
z=y-w^{\alpha}
and obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{C\epsilon y^{\frac{-r}{\alpha}}}{1+y^{\frac{b}{\alpha}}}\int^{3y^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}}_{(y+C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}})^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}}\frac{1}{|y-w^{\alpha}|}\textup{d}w

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\epsilon y^{\frac{1-\alpha-r}{\alpha}}}{1+y^{\frac{b}{\alpha}}}\int_{-(3^{\alpha}-1)y}^{-C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}}\frac{\textup{d}z}{|z|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\epsilon y^{\frac{1-\alpha-r}{\alpha}}}{1+y^{\frac{b}{\alpha}}}\big[\frac{t}{\epsilon}+|\ln{y}|\big].

If
y\geq 1
, then

\displaystyle\frac{C\epsilon y^{\frac{1-\alpha-r}{\alpha}}}{1+y^{\frac{b}{\alpha}}}\big[\frac{t}{\epsilon}+\ln{y}\big]\leq\frac{Cty^{\frac{1-\alpha-r}{\alpha}}}{1+y^{\frac{b}{\alpha}}}+\frac{C\epsilon y^{\frac{1-r}{\alpha}}}{1+y^{\frac{b}{\alpha}}}y^{-1}\ln{y}\leq\frac{C}{1+y^{\frac{b+r-1}{\alpha}}},

where in the last inequality we used the fact that
t\leq 1
.
If
y\leq 1
, we use the fact that
1-2r-\alpha\geq 0
, together with the fact that
y^{r}\ln\frac{1}{y}\leq C
, in order to conclude in the same manner that

\displaystyle\frac{C\epsilon y^{\frac{1-\alpha-r}{\alpha}}}{1+y^{\frac{b}{\alpha}}}\big[\frac{t}{\epsilon}-\ln{y}\big]\leq\frac{Cty^{\frac{1-\alpha-r}{\alpha}}}{1+y^{\frac{b}{\alpha}}}+\frac{C\epsilon y^{\frac{1-\alpha-2r}{\alpha}}}{1+y^{\frac{b}{\alpha}}}y^{r}\ln{\frac{1}{y}}\leq\frac{C}{1+y^{\frac{b+2r-1}{\alpha}}}.

The region
\{y\leq(\frac{w}{3})^{\alpha}\}
follows as in the proof of Proposition
4.3
using in addition the fact that
1-\alpha-r\geq 0
as in (
6.4
). This concludes our proof.

∎

We notice that
H_{\epsilon}
found in Theorem
2.7
satisfies the weak version of (
2.4
).

Weak formulation:
From (
2.4
), it immediately follows that for every
t\in[0,T]
and for every
\varphi\in\textup{C}^{1}_{c}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))
, it holds that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}

\displaystyle H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\varphi(y,v)\textup{d}v\textup{d}y=\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)(v^{\alpha}-y)\partial_{y}\varphi(y,v)\textup{d}v\textup{d}y

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}\int_{(0,\infty)}K(v,w)H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)H_{\epsilon}(y,w,t)\chi_{\varphi}(y,v,w)\textup{d}w\textup{d}v\textup{d}y,

(6.5)

where we denoted

\displaystyle\chi_{\varphi}(y,v,w):=\varphi(y,v+w)-\varphi(y,v)-\varphi(y,w).

We are now in a position to prove Theorem
2.12
.

Proof of Theorem
2.12
.

We first prove that for every fixed
t>0
,
H_{\epsilon}
converges to a Dirac measure.

Convergence to a Dirac measure for fixed time:
If
|y-v^{\alpha}|\geq\delta
and
t>0
, then by (
2.11
)-(
2.13
), it holds that

\displaystyle T_{1}(y,v,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{2A}{1+v^{b}}e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}\psi(e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha}))\mathbbm{1}_{\{y\geq v^{\alpha}-C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}\}}\leq\frac{Ce^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}}{1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}|y-v^{\alpha}|^{m}}\leq C(\delta)e^{-\frac{t(m-1)}{\epsilon}};

\displaystyle T_{2}(y,v,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{2C_{0}^{m-1}\operatorname{max}\{1,A^{2}\}\epsilon}{(1+v^{b})|y-v^{\alpha}|}\mathbbm{1}_{\{(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}\leq y\leq v^{\alpha}-C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}\}}\leq C(\delta)\epsilon;

\displaystyle T_{3}(y,v,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{2}A}{1+v^{b}}e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}\psi(e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha}))\mathbbm{1}_{\{y\leq(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}\}}\leq C(\delta)e^{-\frac{t(m-1)}{\epsilon}}.

Thus, if
t>0
,
\textup{supp}\varphi\subseteq
\{|y-v^{\alpha}|\geq\delta\}
, and
\varphi
is compactly supported, it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\varphi(y,v)\textup{d}v\textup{d}y\leq C(\delta)[\epsilon+e^{-\frac{t(m-1)}{\epsilon}}]\rightarrow 0\textup{ as }\epsilon\rightarrow 0.

(6.6)

We continue by proving equicontinuity in time.

Equicontinuity:
Let
\varphi\in\textup{C}_{0}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))
and
\sigma>0
. We want to prove that for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, there exists
\delta_{1}
such that if
t,s\in[\sigma,T]
with
|t-s|\leq\delta_{1}
, then

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}

\displaystyle[H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)-H_{\epsilon}(y,v,s)]\varphi(y,v)\textup{d}v\textup{d}y\bigg|\leq\delta.

(6.7)

Estimates for the transport term:
We first prove some estimates for the transport term in (
6.5
). Let
\varphi\in\textup{C}^{1}_{c}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))
and
t\geq\sigma
. It holds that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)|y-v^{\alpha}|\partial_{y}\varphi(y,v)\textup{d}v\textup{d}y\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}[T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}](y,v,t)|y-v^{\alpha}|\textup{d}v\textup{d}y.

(6.8)

We estimate first
T_{1}+T_{3}
. We have that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}[T_{1}+T_{3}](y,v,t)|y-v^{\alpha}|\textup{d}v\textup{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}\frac{C|y-v^{\alpha}|}{1+v^{b}}e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}\psi(e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha}))\textup{d}v\textup{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}\frac{Ce^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}|y-v^{\alpha}|}{1+v^{b}}\frac{1}{1+e^{\frac{tm}{\epsilon}}|y-v^{\alpha}|^{m}}\textup{d}v\textup{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}\frac{C}{1+v^{b}}\frac{1}{1+e^{\frac{t(m-1)}{\epsilon}}|y-v^{\alpha}|^{m-1}}\textup{d}v\textup{d}y.

(6.9)

We make the change of variables
\xi=e^{\frac{t}{\epsilon}}(y-v^{\alpha})
. (
6.9
) then becomes for all
t\geq\sigma

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}[T_{1}+T_{3}](y,v,t)|y-v^{\alpha}|\textup{d}v\textup{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}\frac{C}{1+v^{b}}\frac{e^{-\frac{t}{\epsilon}}}{1+|\xi|^{m-1}}\textup{d}v\textup{d}\xi

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{\sigma}{\epsilon}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}\frac{C}{1+v^{b}}\frac{1}{1+|\xi|^{m-1}}\textup{d}v\textup{d}\xi\leq Ce^{-\frac{\sigma}{\epsilon}}.

We continue by estimating
T_{2}
. In this case, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}T_{2}(y,v,t)|y-v^{\alpha}|\textup{d}v\textup{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}\frac{C\epsilon|y-v^{\alpha}|}{(1+v^{b})|y-v^{\alpha}|}\mathbbm{1}_{\{(\frac{v}{3})^{\alpha}\leq y\leq v^{\alpha}\}}\textup{d}v\textup{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\int_{(0,\infty)}\frac{C\epsilon}{1+v^{b-\alpha}}\textup{d}v\leq C\epsilon.

Combining all the above estimates, we have that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)|y-v^{\alpha}|\partial_{y}\varphi(y,v)\textup{d}v\textup{d}y\leq Ce^{-\frac{\sigma}{\epsilon}}+C\epsilon.

(6.10)

Equicontinuity, test functions in
\textup{C}^{1}_{c}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))
:
Let
M>1
. We first prove (
6.7
) for a fixed function
\varphi\in\textup{C}^{1}_{c}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))
such that
\textup{supp}\varphi\in\{[-M,M]\times[\frac{1}{M},M]\}
,
||\varphi||_{L^{\infty}}\leq 1
. Let
s,t\in[\sigma,T]
. By (
6.5
), it holds that

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}

\displaystyle[H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)-H_{\epsilon}(y,v,s)]\varphi(y,v)\textup{d}v\textup{d}y\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}H_{\epsilon}(y,v,z)|v^{\alpha}-y||\partial_{y}\varphi(y,v)|\textup{d}v\textup{d}y\textup{d}z

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}\int_{(0,\infty)}K(v,w)H_{\epsilon}(y,v,z)H_{\epsilon}(y,w,z)|\chi_{\varphi}(y,v,w)|\textup{d}w\textup{d}v\textup{d}y\textup{d}z

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}H_{\epsilon}(y,v,z)|v^{\alpha}-y||\partial_{y}\varphi(y,v)|\textup{d}v\textup{d}y\textup{d}z

\displaystyle+C\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}(1+v^{\gamma})H_{\epsilon}(y,v,z)\textup{d}v\int_{(0,\infty)}(1+w^{\gamma})H_{\epsilon}(y,w,z)\textup{d}w\textup{d}y\textup{d}z

\displaystyle=:J_{1}+J_{2}.

By (
6.10
) and since
t,s\geq\sigma
, it follows that

\displaystyle J_{1}\leq\frac{C}{\epsilon}[e^{-\frac{\sigma}{\epsilon}}+\epsilon]|t-s|\leq C|t-s|,

(6.11)

for all
\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{\sigma}
, where
\epsilon_{\sigma}
only depends on
\sigma
.
By (
6.1
) it follows that

\displaystyle J_{2}\leq\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{C}{1+|y|^{2\omega}}\textup{d}y\textup{d}z\leq C|t-s|.

(6.12)

By (
6.11
) and (
6.12
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}

\displaystyle[H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)-H_{\epsilon}(y,v,s)]\varphi(y,v)\textup{d}v\textup{d}y\bigg|\leq C|t-s|,

(6.13)

when
\varphi\in\textup{C}^{1}_{c}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))
.

From
\textup{C}^{1}_{c}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))
to
\textup{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty)):
Let
\varphi\in\textup{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))
such that
\textup{supp}\varphi\in[-M,M]\times[\frac{1}{M},M]
. There exists
\varphi_{n}\in\textup{C}^{1}_{c}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))
such that
||\varphi-\varphi_{n}||_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{n}
. It then holds that

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}

\displaystyle[H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)-H_{\epsilon}(y,v,s)]\varphi(y,v)\textup{d}v\textup{d}y\bigg|\leq\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}[H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)-H_{\epsilon}(y,v,s)]\varphi_{n}(y,v)\textup{d}v\textup{d}y\bigg|

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}[H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+H_{\epsilon}(y,v,s)]|\varphi(y,v)-\varphi_{n}(y,v)|\textup{d}v\textup{d}y.

By (
6.13
), it follows that

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}

\displaystyle[H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)-H_{\epsilon}(y,v,s)]\varphi(y,v)\textup{d}v\textup{d}y\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C|t-s|+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}[H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+H_{\epsilon}(y,v,s)]\textup{d}v\textup{d}y\leq C|t-s|+\frac{C}{n}.

(6.14)

We can then conclude by choosing
n\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large.

From
\textup{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))
to
\textup{C}_{0}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty)):

Let
\varphi\in\textup{C}_{0}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))
. Let
M>1
, then

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}

\displaystyle[H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)-H_{\epsilon}(y,v,s)]\varphi(y,v)\textup{d}v\textup{d}y\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\int_{[-M,M]}\int_{[\frac{1}{M},M]}[H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)-H_{\epsilon}(y,v,s)]\varphi(y,v)\textup{d}v\textup{d}y\bigg|

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{v\geq M\}\cup\{v\leq\frac{1}{M}\}}[H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+H_{\epsilon}(y,v,s)]|\varphi(y,v)-\varphi_{n}(y,v)|\textup{d}v\textup{d}y

\displaystyle+\int_{|y|\geq M}\int_{(M,\infty)}[H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+H_{\epsilon}(y,v,s)]|\varphi(y,v)-\varphi_{n}(y,v)|\textup{d}v\textup{d}y=:L_{1}+L_{2}+L_{3}.

We have that the following inequalities hold by choosing
M
sufficiently large.

\displaystyle L_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta}{3}\qquad\textup{ by }\eqref{equi compact supp};

\displaystyle L_{2}

\displaystyle\leq M^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{v\geq M\}}v[H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+H_{\epsilon}(y,v,s)]\textup{d}v\textup{d}y

\displaystyle+M^{-r}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{v\leq\frac{1}{M}\}}v^{-r}[H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+H_{\epsilon}(y,v,s)]\textup{d}v\textup{d}y\leq\frac{\delta}{3}\qquad\textup{ by }\eqref{integral in y};

\displaystyle L_{3}

\displaystyle\leq M^{-1}\int_{|y|\geq M}\int_{(M,\infty)}|y|[H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)+H_{\epsilon}(y,v,s)]\textup{d}v\textup{d}y\leq\frac{\delta}{3}\qquad\textup{ by }\eqref{integral large y}.

This concludes the proof of equicontinuity. Similar computations for the passage from
\varphi\in\textup{C}^{1}_{c}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))
to
\varphi\in\textup{C}_{0}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))
have also been used in
[
10
]
.

Convergence to a Dirac measure:

By (
6.2
), we have that for
t\in[0,T]
, there exists a convergent subsequence of
H_{\epsilon}(t)
in the weak-
∗
topology as
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
. Let
\sigma>0
. Together with (
6.7
), we deduce from Arzelà–Ascoli theorem that there exists a subsequence of
\{H_{\epsilon}\}
, which we do not relabel, and an
H\in\textup{C}([\sigma,T];\mathscr{M}_{+}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))),
such that
H_{\epsilon}(t)
converge to
H(t)
in the weak-
∗
topology as
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
, for every
t\in[\sigma,T].

By (
6.6
), we have that for every
t>0
and
\varphi
such that
\textup{supp}\varphi\subseteq
\{|y-v^{\alpha}|\geq\delta\}
,
\varphi
is compactly supported, it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\varphi(y,v)\textup{d}v\textup{d}y\rightarrow 0\textup{ as }\epsilon\rightarrow 0.

(6.15)

The extension to functions
\varphi\in\textup{C}_{0}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))
is as before. In other words, for any
\delta>0
and for every
\varphi\in\textup{C}_{0}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))
such that
\textup{supp}\varphi\subseteq\{|y-v^{\alpha}|\geq\delta\}
, we have that

\displaystyle 0=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}H_{\epsilon}(y,v,t)\varphi(y,v)\textup{d}v\textup{d}y=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(0,\infty)}H(y,v,t)\varphi(y,v)\textup{d}v\textup{d}y.

This concludes our proof.
∎

Appendix A
Rescaled version of the results

Existence of mass-conserving solutions has been proved in
[
9
]
for the rain model (
1.4
) for times of order one. Theorem
2.7
extends the existence of mass-conserving solutions in
[
9
]
to hold for times of order
\mathcal{O}(e^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}})
in the case of coagulation kernels with negligible contribution. More precisely, making use of (
1.14
), (
1.15
), Table
1
, and Theorem
2.7
, we obtain that

Proposition A.1
.

Let
H_{\epsilon,\textup{in}}
be as in Assumption
2.1
and
K
as in Assumption
2.2
. Let
b\geq\operatorname{max}\{\overline{b}(\gamma,\alpha),2\gamma+1\}
and
m>\operatorname{max}\{\frac{2(\gamma+1)}{\alpha},\frac{b}{\alpha}+1\}
, where
\overline{b}(\gamma,\alpha)
is as in (
2.18
). There exist
M_{1}(\alpha,\gamma,b,m),M_{2}(\alpha,\gamma,b,m),
depending only on
\alpha,\gamma,b,m
, such that for any
A\geq 1
, there exist
\overline{\epsilon}\in(0,1)
, sufficiently small, and
T>0
, sufficiently small, which is independent of
\epsilon\in(0,1),
such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\overline{\epsilon}]
there exists a mass-conserving mild solution
f_{\epsilon}\in\textup{C}([0,e^{\frac{T}{\epsilon}}-1];L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty)))
to (
1.4
). Moreover, we have that
f_{\epsilon}
satisfies the following estimates for
C_{0}
as in (
2.9
).

If
v^{\alpha}(\tau+1)\big[1-(\frac{1}{3})^{\alpha}\big]\geq C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}
, then

\displaystyle 0\leq(1+v^{b})f_{\epsilon}(y,v,\tau)\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2A}{1+|x-(\tau+1)v^{\alpha}|^{m}}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{\epsilon,y,v,\tau}}+\frac{2M_{1}A^{3}\epsilon}{|x-(\tau+1)v^{\alpha}|}\mathbbm{1}_{\big(A_{\epsilon,y,v,\tau}\cup B_{\epsilon,y,v,\tau}\big)^{c}}

(A.1)

\displaystyle+\frac{M_{2}A}{1+|x-(\tau+1)v^{\alpha}|^{m}}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{\epsilon,y,v,\tau}},

where

\displaystyle A_{\epsilon,y,v,\tau}

\displaystyle:=\{x-(\tau+1)v^{\alpha}\geq-C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}\};

\displaystyle B_{\epsilon,y,v,\tau}

\displaystyle:=\bigg\{\frac{x}{\tau+1}\leq\Big(\frac{v}{3}\Big)^{\alpha}\bigg\}.

Otherwise, if
0\leq v^{\alpha}(\tau+1)\big[1-(\frac{1}{3})^{\alpha}\big]\leq C_{0}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}
, then

\displaystyle 0\leq(1+v^{b})f_{\epsilon}(x,v,\tau)\leq\frac{2A}{1+|x-(\tau+1)v^{\alpha}|^{m}}.

(A.2)

With the form of the bounds in (
A.1
), the reason behind the choice of (
2.19
) becomes clear. More precisely,

Remark A.2
.

Denote by
r(x,v,\tau):=\frac{1}{1+|x-\tau v^{\alpha}|^{m}+v^{p}}
and by
p=\alpha m
. Then

\displaystyle\partial_{v}r(x,v,\tau)=-\frac{pv^{p-1}-p(x-v^{\alpha}\tau)^{m-1}v^{\alpha-1}\tau}{(1+|x-\tau v^{\alpha}|^{m}+v^{p})^{2}}.

In other words,
\partial_{v}r(x,v,\tau)=0
if and only if
x\tau^{\frac{1}{m-1}}=v^{\alpha}(1+\tau^{\frac{m}{m-1}})
. Thus, for fixed
x
and
\tau
, the point where the function
r
changes its sign can be approximated for large times by
v^{\alpha}\approx\frac{x}{\tau}
and for small times by
v^{\alpha}\approx x\tau^{\frac{1}{m-1}}
. The coagulation operator can be approximated at a formal level by a term of the form
v^{\gamma}\partial_{v}r(x,v,\tau)\int_{0}^{\infty}r(x,v^{\prime},\tau)\textup{d}v^{\prime}
. The change of sign of
r
was dealt with in the proof made in
[
9
]
by taking a cut-off at
v^{\alpha}_{\textup{max}}\approx x\tau^{\frac{1}{m-1}}.
While at a first glance there is no similarity between the methods to prove existence in
[
9
]
with the methods in the current paper, the bounds in (
2.19
) and (
A.1
), respectively, can be understood at an intuitive level as taking a cut-off at
v^{\alpha}\approx\frac{x}{\tau}
. Thus, our existence result can be understood as the natural continuation of the proof in
[
9
]
for large times.

Appendix B
Construction of solutions with compact support

We now prove that
H_{n+1}
defined in (
2.51
) has compact support assuming that it has compact support at initial time. This is in order to find a time
\tilde{\delta}
as in Proposition
3.1
that is independent of
y
and
v
.

Proof of Proposition
2.17
.

We first analyze the following equation.

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f_{n+1}(y,v,t)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}v^{\alpha}\partial_{x}f(x,v,t)=

\displaystyle\mathbb{K}[f_{n+1},f_{n}](y,v,t),

(B.1)

where
\mathbb{K}_{N}[f_{n+1},f_{n}]
is as in (
2.55
). Existence of
f_{n}\in C^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))
for small times independent of
\epsilon
follows via Banach’s fixed point theorem.

We then define

\displaystyle H_{n}(y,v,\tau):=e^{\tau}f_{n}(x,v,t),\quad x:=e^{\tau}y,\quad t:=e^{\tau}-1,\textup{ for all }n\in\mathbb{N}.

Thus,
H_{n}\in C^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty))
and solves (
2.55
). Compact support follows directly from (
2.55
) and the choice of
K_{N}
in (
2.56
).

∎
